                                                                                                                                       IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
IFRS 3          Business Combinations                                                                                                  IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview        An acquirer of a business recognises the assets acquired and liabilities assumed at their acquisition-date             IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                fair values and discloses information that enables users to evaluate the nature and financial effects of the           IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                acquisition.
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
Business        A business combination is a transaction or event in which an acquirer obtains control of one or more businesses.
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
combination
                A business is defined as an integrated set of activities and assets that is capable of being conducted and managed      IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                for the purpose of providing goods or services to customers, generating investment income (such as dividends or
                                                                                                                                       IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                interest) or generating other income from ordinary activities.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
Recognition     The acquisition method is used for all business combinations.
of assets and                                                                                                                          IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
liabilities     The acquirer recognises the identifiable assets acquired, the liabilities assumed and any non-controlling
                                                                                                                                       IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                interest (NCI) in the acquiree.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                Intangible assets, including in-process research and development, acquired in a business combination
                                                                                                                                       IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                are recognised separately from goodwill if they arise as a result of contractual or legal rights, or if they are
                separable from the business. In these circumstances the recognition criteria are always considered to be               IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                satisfied (see also IAS 38).                                                                                          IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2



                                                                                                                                                           55
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                                 Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                            Deloitte
                                                                                                                                             IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                                  Accounting
                                                                                                                                             Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                             Standards,
                                                                                                                                               Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                             (DART)
                                                                                                                                                                 Practice
                                                                                                                                             Statements
                                                                                                                                               Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                               IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Measurement          Assets and liabilities are measured at their fair values (with a limited number of specified exceptions) at the
                     date the entity obtains control of the acquiree. If the initial accounting for a business combination can be              IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                     determined only provisionally by the end of the first reporting period, the combination is accounted for using            IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                     provisional values. Adjustments to provisional values relating to facts and circumstances that existed at the             IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                     acquisition date are permitted within one year.
                                                                                                                                               IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                     The acquirer can elect to measure the components of NCI in the acquiree that are present ownership                        IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                     interests and entitle their holders to a proportionate share of the entity’s net assets in liquidation either at fair
                                                                                                                                                IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                     value or at the NCI’s proportionate share of the net assets.
Contingent           Among the items recognised will be the acquisition-date fair value of contingent consideration. Changes to                IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
consideration        contingent consideration resulting from events after the acquisition date are recognised in profit or loss.               IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                                                                                                                                               IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
Goodwill and      If the consideration transferred exceeds the net of the assets, liabilities and NCI, that excess is recognised as
                                                                                                                                               IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
bargain purchases goodwill. If the consideration is lower than the net assets acquired, a bargain purchase is recognised in profit
                  or loss.                                                                                                                     IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36

Acquisition costs    All acquisition-related costs (e.g. finder’s fees, professional or consulting fees, costs of internal acquisition         IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                     department) are recognised in profit or loss except for costs to issue debt or equity, which are recognised in            IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                     accordance with IFRS 9 and IAS 32.
                                                                                                                                              IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

combinations         held equity interest is remeasured at acquisition-date fair value and any resulting gain or loss is recognised in     IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
achieved in stages   profit or loss.                                                                                                       IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10

                                                                                                                                           IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16

Pending changes In March 2020, the IASB published DP/2020/1 Business Combinations—Disclosures, Goodwill and Impairment.
                In the DP, the IASB is proposing to develop enhanced disclosure requirements to improve the information               IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                entities provide to investors about the businesses those entities buy. This includes proposals to require             IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                entities to disclose management’s objectives for acquisitions in the year of acquisition and how acquisitions         IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                have performed against those objectives in subsequent periods.
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                    The IASB is also proposing that amortisation of goodwill should not be reintroduced.                              IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2

                    To simplify the impairment test, the IASB’s view is that amendments should be proposed to provide relief from      IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                    the annual impairment test of cash-generating units containing goodwill if there are no impairment indicators     IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                    and simplify how value in use is estimated.
                                                                                                                                      IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                    An exposure draft is expected in the first half of 2024.                                                          IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27

                    In November 2020, the IASB published DP/2020/2 Business Combinations under Common Control. The DP                 IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                    examines how to account for business combinations in which all of the combining businesses are ultimately         IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                    controlled by the same party, both before and after the combination.
                                                                                                                                      IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                    The IASB is expected to decide the direction of the project in September 2023.                                    IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12

IFRS 5            Non-current Assets Held for Sale and Discontinued Operations                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview          Sets out the accounting for non-current assets held for sale and the presentation and disclosure of                            IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                  discontinued operations.                                                                                                       IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
Non-current       Non-current assets are ‘held for sale’ either individually or as part of a disposal group when the entity has the              IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
assets held for   intention to sell them, they are available for immediate sale and disposal within 12 months is highly probable.
                                                                                                                                                 IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
sale
                  A disposal group is a group of assets to be disposed of in a single transaction, including any related liabilities that         IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                  will also be transferred.
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                  Assets and liabilities of a subsidiary are classified as held for sale if the parent is committed to a plan involving loss     IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                  of control of the subsidiary, regardless of whether the entity will retain a non-controlling interest after the sale.
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                  IFRS 5 applies to a non-current asset (or disposal group) that is classified as held for distribution to owners.               IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
Discontinued      A discontinued operation is a component of an entity that has either been disposed of or is classified as                      IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
operations        held for sale. It must represent a separate major line of business or major geographical area of operations,
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                  be part of a single co-ordinated plan to dispose of a separate major line of business or geographical area of
                  operations.                                                                                                                    IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                                IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                         Introduction

                                                                                                                                       Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Measurement          Non-current assets ‘held for sale’ are measured at the lower of the carrying amount and fair value less costs
                     to sell (or costs to distribute). The non-current assets are no longer depreciated.                               IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                     Immediately before the initial classification of the asset (or disposal group) as held for sale, the carrying
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                     amounts of the assets (or all the assets and liabilities in the group) are measured in accordance with
                     applicable IFRS Standards.                                                                                        IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
Statement of         When there are discontinued operations, the statement of comprehensive income is divided into continuing          IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
comprehensive        and discontinued operations.                                                                                       IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
income
                     The sum of the post-tax profit or loss from discontinued operations for the period and the post-tax gain          IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                     or loss arising on the disposal of discontinued operations (or on their reclassification as held for sale) is     IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                     presented as a single amount.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
Statement of         Non-current assets, and the assets and liabilities in a disposal group, are presented separately in the
financial position   statement of financial position.                                                                                  IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                                                                                                                                       IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
Relationship with    IFRS 5 has its own disclosure requirements. Consequently, disclosures in other Standards do not apply to          IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
other Standards      such assets (or disposal groups) unless those Standards specifically require disclosures or the disclosures
                                                                                                                                       IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                     relate to the measurement of assets or liabilities within a disposal group that are outside the scope of the
                     measurement requirements of IFRS 5.                                                                              IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

IFRS 6              Exploration for and Evaluation of Mineral Resources                                                                          IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview            Prescribes the financial reporting for the exploration for and evaluation of mineral resources until the IASB                IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                    completes a comprehensive project in this area.                                                                              IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
Continued use of    An entity can continue to use its existing accounting policies provided that they result in information that is reliable     IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
existing policies   and is relevant to the economic decision-making needs of users. It does not require or prohibit any specific
                                                                                                                                                 IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                    accounting policies for the recognition and measurement of exploration and evaluation assets.
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                    The Standard gives a temporary exemption from applying IAS 8:11-12— which specify a hierarchy of sources of
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                    authoritative guidance in the absence of a specific IFRS Standard.
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
Impairment          Exploration and evaluation assets must be assessed for impairment when there is an indication that their
                    carrying amount exceeds their recoverable amount. Exploration and evaluation assets must also be tested                      IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                    for impairment before they are reclassified as development assets.                                                           IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32

                    IFRS 6 allows impairment to be assessed at a level higher than the ‘cash-generating unit’ under IAS 36, but                  IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                    requires measurement of the impairment in accordance with IAS 36 once it is assessed.                                        IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
Disclosure          IFRS 6 requires disclosure of information that identifies and explains amounts arising from exploration and                  IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                    evaluation of mineral resources.

IFRS 7               Financial Instruments: Disclosures                                                                                       IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview             Prescribes disclosures to help the primary users of the financial statements evaluate the significance of                IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                     financial instruments to the entity, the nature and extent of their risks and how the entity manages those               IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                     risks.
                                                                                                                                              IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
Significance         Requires disclosure of information about the significance of financial instruments to an entity’s financial position
                                                                                                                                              IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
of financial         and performance, including its accounting policies and application of hedge accounting.
instruments                                                                                                                                    IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10

Financial position   Entities must disclose information about financial assets and financial liabilities by category; special                 IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                     disclosures when the fair value option or fair value through OCI option is used; reclassifications; offsetting of        IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                     financial assets and liabilities; collateral; allowance accounts; compound financial instruments with embedded
                                                                                                                                              IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                     derivatives; defaults and breaches and transfers of financial assets.
Financial            Information must be disclosed about financial instruments-related recognised income, expenses, gains and                 IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
performance          losses; interest income and expense; fee income; and impairment losses.                                                  IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36

Other disclosures   The significant accounting policies on financial instruments must be disclosed. When hedge accounting is
                    applied, extensive information about the risk management strategy, the amount, timing and uncertainty             IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                    of future cash flows and the effects of hedge accounting on financial position and performance must be            IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                    disclosed. This information is required regardless of whether an entity has applied hedge accounting in           IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                    accordance with IAS 39 or IFRS 9. Fair values must be disclosed for each class of financial instrument and
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                    IFRS 13 also requires information to be disclosed about the fair values.
Risk                Entities must disclose the nature and extent of risks arising from financial instruments. This includes           IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                    qualitative information about exposures to each class of risk and how those risks are managed and                  IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                    quantitative information about exposures to each class of risk. Extensive disclosures are required for credit     IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                    risk to assess expected credit losses. This includes reconciliations of the loss allowance and gross carrying
                    amounts and information about credit quality. Additional disclosure requirements relate to liquidity risk and     IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                    market risk (including sensitivity analyses for market risk).                                                     IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27

                     measured at amortised cost or FVTOCI and to each class of financial liability measured at amortised cost.

IFRS 8       Operating Segments                                                                                                     IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview     Requires entities to disclose segmental information that is consistent with how it is reported internally to the       IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
             chief operating decision maker.                                                                                        IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
Scope        This Standard applies only to entities with debt or equity instruments traded in a public market or that is in the     IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
             process of issuing instruments in a public market.
                                                                                                                                    IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
Operating    An operating segment is a component of an entity that engages in business activities from which it may earn
                                                                                                                                     IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
segments     revenues and incur expenses, whose operating results are regularly reviewed by the entity’s chief operating
             decision maker and for which discrete financial information is available.                                              IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                                                                                                                                    IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
             Generally, separate information is required if the revenue, profit or loss, or assets of a segment are 10 per
             cent or more of the equivalent total for all of the operating segments.                                                IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                                                                                                                                    IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
             At least 75 per cent of the entity’s revenue must be included in reportable segments.
                                                                                                                                    IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
Disclosure   A measure of profit or loss and a measure of total assets and liabilities must be presented for each reportable
             segment. Additional measures such as revenue from external customers, interest revenue and expense,                    IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
             depreciation and amortisation expense and tax is required to be presented if they are included in the                  IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
             measure of profit or loss reviewed by the chief operating decision maker or provided to them separately.
                                                                                                                                   IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2
             The segment information needs not be prepared in conformity with the accounting policies adopted for the
             entity’s financial statements.
                                                                                                                                                        68
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                        Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                   Deloitte
                                                                                                                                    IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                         Accounting
                                                                                                                                    Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                    Standards,

                                                                                                                                                    (DART)
                                                                                                                                                        Practice
                                                                                                                                    Statements
                                                                                                                                      Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Entity-wide         Some entity-wide disclosures are required even when an entity has only one reportable segment. These
disclosures         include information about each product and service or groups of products and services, geographical areas,        IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                    major customers (10 per cent or more of the entity’s revenue) and judgements made by management in                IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                    applying the aggregation criteria for operating segments.                                                         IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                    Analyses of revenues and some non-current assets by geographical area are required from all entities—with         IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                    an expanded requirement to disclose revenues/non-current assets by individual foreign country (if material),      IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                    irrespective of how the entity is organised.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
Reconciliation      A reconciliation of the total assets to the entity’s assets should only be provided if the segment assets are
                    regularly provided to the chief operating decision maker.                                                         IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19

Interpretations     None

year

IFRS 9        Financial Instruments                                                                                                        IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview      Sets out requirements for recognition and measurement of financial instruments, including impairment,                        IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
              derecognition and general hedge accounting.                                                                                  IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
Initial       All financial instruments are initially measured at fair value plus or minus, in the case of a financial asset or            IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
measurement   financial liability not at fair value through profit or loss, transaction costs.
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
Equity        Equity investments held are measured at fair value. Changes in the fair value are recognised in profit or loss                IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
investments   (FVTPL). However, if an equity investment is not held for trading, an entity can make an irrevocable election at initial     IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
              recognition to recognise the fair value changes in OCI (FVTOCI) with only dividend income recognised in profit or
              loss. There is no reclassification to profit or loss on disposal. The impairment requirements do not apply to equity         IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
              instruments.                                                                                                                 IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27

Classification of   Financial assets with contractual terms that give rise on specified dates to cash flows that are solely payments
financial assets    of principal and interest (SPPI) on the principal amount outstanding (the contractual cash flows test) are             IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                    classified according to the objective of the business model of the entity.                                             IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                    If the objective is to hold the financial assets to collect the contractual cash flows, they are measured at
                    amortised cost, unless the entity applies the fair value option. Interest revenue is calculated by applying the        IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                    effective interest rate to the amortised cost (which is the gross carrying amount minus any loss allowance)            IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                    for credit-impaired financial assets while for all other instruments, it is calculated based on the gross carrying
                                                                                                                                            IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                    amount.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                    If the objective is to both collect contractual cash flows and sell financial assets, they are measured at FVTOCI
                                                                                                                                           IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                    (with reclassification to profit or loss on disposal), unless the entity applies the fair value option.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                    All other financial assets must be measured at fair value through profit or loss (FVTPL).
                                                                                                                                           IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
Fair value option   An entity may, at initial recognition, irrevocably designate a financial asset as measured at FVTPL if doing so
                                                                                                                                           IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                    eliminates or significantly reduces a measurement or recognition inconsistency (accounting mismatch) that
                    would otherwise arise from measuring assets or liabilities or recognising the gains and losses on them on              IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                    different bases.                                                                                                       IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                          IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

                                                                                                                                                  IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Financial liabilities   Financial liabilities held for trading are measured at FVTPL.
                                                                                                                                                  IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                        All other financial liabilities are measured at amortised cost unless the fair value option is applied. The fair          IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                        value option can be elected at initial recognition if doing so eliminates or significantly reduces an accounting
                                                                                                                                                  IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                        mismatch. In addition, financial liabilities can be designated as at FVTPL if a group of financial instruments is
                        managed on a fair value basis or if the designation is made in relation to embedded derivatives that would                IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                        otherwise be bifurcated from the liability host.                                                                          IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2

                        Changes in fair value attributable to changes in credit risk of the liability designated as at FVTPL are presented         IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                        in OCI (and there is no reclassification to profit or loss).                                                              IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
Derivatives             All derivatives in the scope of IFRS 9, including those linked to unquoted equity investments, are measured               IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                        at fair value. Value changes are recognised in profit or loss unless the entity has elected to apply hedge
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                        accounting by designating the derivative as a hedging instrument in an eligible hedging relationship.
Embedded                The contractual cash flows of a financial asset are assessed in their entirety, including those of an embedded            IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
derivatives             derivative that is not closely related to its host. The financial asset as a whole is measured at FVTPL if the            IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                        contractual cash flow characteristics test is not passed.                                                                 IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                        For financial liabilities, an embedded derivative not closely related to its host is accounted for separately at fair     IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                        value in the case of financial liabilities not designated at FVTPL.                                                      IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

                        For other non-financial asset host contracts, an embedded derivative not closely related to its host is
                        accounted for separately at fair value.

Hedge accounting   The hedge accounting requirements in IFRS 9 are optional. If the eligibility and qualification criteria are
                   met, hedge accounting allows an entity to reflect risk management activities in the financial statements by             IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                   matching gains or losses on hedging instruments with losses or gains on the risk exposures they hedge.                  IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                   There are three types of hedging relationships: (i) fair value hedge; (ii) cash flow hedge and (iii) hedge of a net
                   investment in a foreign operation.                                                                                      IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                   A hedging relationship qualifies for hedge accounting only if the hedging relationship consists only of
                   eligible hedging instruments and eligible hedged items, the hedging relationship is formally designated and              IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                   documented (including the entity’s risk management objective and strategy for undertaking the hedge) at                 IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                   inception and the hedging relationship is effective.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                   To be effective there must be an economic relationship between the hedged item and the hedging                          IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                   instrument, the effect of credit risk must not dominate the value changes that result from that economic
                                                                                                                                           IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                   relationship and the hedge ratio of the hedging relationship must be the same as that actually used in the
                   economic hedge.                                                                                                         IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                                                                                                                                           IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                                                                                                                                           IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                          IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

Impairment   The impairment model in IFRS 9 is based on expected credit losses. It applies to financial assets measured at
             amortised cost or FVTOCI, lease receivables, contract assets within the scope of IFRS 15 and specified written         IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
             loan commitments (unless measured at FVTPL) and financial guarantee contracts (unless they are accounted               IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
             for in accordance with IFRS 4 or IFRS 17).                                                                             IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
             Expected credit losses (with the exception of purchased or original credit-impaired financial assets) are              IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
             required to be measured through a loss allowance at an amount equal to the 12-month expected credit                    IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
             losses. If the credit risk has increased significantly since initial recognition of the financial instrument, full
                                                                                                                                     IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
             lifetime expected credit losses are recognised. This is equally true for credit-impaired financial assets for
             which interest income is based on amortised cost rather than gross carrying amount.                                    IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                                                                                                                                    IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
             IFRS 9 requires expected credit losses to reflect an unbiased and probability-weighted amount, the time value
             of money and reasonable and supportable information about past events, current conditions and forecasts                IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
             of future economic conditions.                                                                                         IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                                                                                                                                    IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                                                                                                                                    IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                                                                                                                                    IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12

Interpretations   IFRIC 16 Hedges of a Net Investment in a Foreign Operation clarifies that the presentation currency does not
                  create an exposure to which an entity may apply hedge accounting. A parent entity may designate as a hedged             IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                  risk only the foreign exchange differences arising from a difference between its own functional currency and            IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                  that of its foreign operation.                                                                                          IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                  The hedging instrument(s) can be held by any entity within the group as long as the designation, effectiveness          IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                  and documentation requirements are satisfied.                                                                           IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2

                  On derecognition of a foreign operation, IFRS 9 must be applied to determine the amount that needs to                    IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                  be reclassified to profit or loss from the foreign currency translation reserve in respect of the hedging               IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                  instrument, while IAS 21 must be applied in respect of the hedged item.
                                                                                                                                          IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                  IFRIC 19 Extinguishing Financial Liabilities with Equity Instruments clarifies that when a borrower agrees with         IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                  a lender to issue equity instruments to the lender to extinguish all or part of a financial liability, the issue of
                                                                                                                                          IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                  equity instruments is the consideration paid. Those equity instruments issued must be measured at their
                  fair value on the date of extinguishment of the liability. If that fair value is not reliably measurable they are       IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                  measured using the fair value of the liability extinguished.                                                            IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39

IFRS 10    Consolidated Financial Statements                                                                                               IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview                                                                                                                                   IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
           Sets out the requirements for determining whether an entity (a parent) controls another entity (a subsidiary).
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
Control    An investor controls an investee when it has power over the investee, exposure, or rights, to variable returns from
           its involvement with the investee and the ability to use its power over the investee to affect the amount of the                IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
           returns.                                                                                                                        IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2

           An investor has power when it has existing rights that give it the current ability to direct the relevant activities of the      IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
           investee—the activities that significantly affect the investee’s returns.                                                       IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19

           Sometimes assessing power is straightforward, such as when power over an investee is obtained directly and                      IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
           solely from the voting rights granted by equity instruments such as shares, and can be assessed by considering the              IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
           voting rights from those shareholdings. It is possible to have control with less than half the voting rights (sometimes         IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
           referred to as de-facto control).
                                                                                                                                           IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
           In other cases, the assessment will be more complex and require more than one factor to be considered, for                      IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
           example when power results from one or more contractual arrangements.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                          IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2



                                                                                                                                                               79
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                         Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                    Deloitte
                                                                                                                                     IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                          Accounting
                                                                                                                                     Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                     Standards,
                                                                                                                                       Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                     (DART)
                                                                                                                                                         Practice
                                                                                                                                     Statements
                                                                                                                                       Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
               The Standard includes guidance on distinguishing between rights that give the holder power and rights that are
               intended to protect the investor’s interest in the entity. Protective rights might include a right to vote on major     IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
               transactions such as significant asset purchases or to approve borrowings above a specified level. Distinguishing       IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
               between rights that give power and rights that are protective requires an understanding of the relevant activities      IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
               of the entity.
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
               Sometimes an entity will delegate its power to an agent. The Standard emphasises the importance of identifying          IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
               when a party that appears to have control over an entity is only exercising power as an agent or a principal.
                                                                                                                                        IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
Consolidated   When a parent-subsidiary relationship exists, consolidated financial statements are required. These are
financial      financial statements of a group (parent and subsidiaries) presented as those of a single economic entity.               IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
statements                                                                                                                             IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
               There are two exceptions to this requirement. If, on acquisition, a subsidiary meets the criteria to be classified
                                                                                                                                       IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
               as held for sale in accordance with IFRS 5, it is accounted for under that Standard. The other exception is for
               investment entities.                                                                                                    IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
Investment     An entity that obtains funds from one or more investors for the purpose of providing those investor(s) with             IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
entities       investment management services; commits to its investor(s) that its business purpose is to invest funds                 IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
               solely for returns from capital appreciation, investment income, or both; and measures and evaluates the
                                                                                                                                       IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
               performance of substantially all of its investments on a fair value basis is an investment entity.
                                                                                                                                      IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2
               An investment entity does not consolidate its subsidiaries. Instead it measures the investment at fair value
               through profit or loss in accordance with IFRS 9.

IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                          Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                     Deloitte

                                                                                                                                                      Standards,

                                                                                                                                        Our IAS Plus website

Consolidation     Intragroup balances, transactions, income and expenses are eliminated.
procedures                                                                                                                              IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                  All entities in the group use the same accounting policies and, if practicable, the same reporting date.              IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                  Non-controlling interests (NCI) are reported in equity separately from the equity of the owners of the parent.        IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                  Total comprehensive income is allocated between NCI and the owners of the parent even if this results in the          IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                  NCI having a deficit balance.
                                                                                                                                        IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
Changes in        A change in the ownership interest of a subsidiary, when control is retained, is accounted for as an equity
                                                                                                                                         IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
the ownership     transaction and no gain or loss is recognised.
interest                                                                                                                                IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                  Partial disposal of an investment in a subsidiary that results in loss of control triggers remeasurement of the
                                                                                                                                        IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                  residual holding to fair value at the date control is lost. Any difference between fair value and carrying amount
                  is a gain or loss on the disposal, recognised in profit or loss.                                                      IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
Interpretations   None                                                                                                                  IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32

year                                                                                                                                   IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

IFRS 11       Joint Arrangements                                                                                                      IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview      Sets out principles for identifying whether an entity has a joint arrangement, and if it does whether it is a joint     IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
              venture or joint operation.                                                                                             IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
Definitions   A joint arrangement is one in which two or more parties have joint control over activities.                             IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16

              A joint venture is a joint arrangement in which the venturers have rights to the net assets of the venture.             IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                                                                                                                                       IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
              A joint operation is a joint arrangement whereby each joint operator has rights to assets and obligations for the
              liabilities of the operation.                                                                                           IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                                                                                                                                      IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
              The distinction between a joint operation and a joint venture requires assessment of the structure of the joint
              arrangement, the legal form of any separate vehicle, the terms of the contractual arrangement and any other             IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
              relevant facts and circumstances.                                                                                       IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
Accounting    A joint venturer applies the equity method, as described in IAS 28, except joint ventures where the investor is         IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
              a venture capital firm, mutual fund or unit trust, and it elects or is required to measure such investments at
                                                                                                                                      IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
              fair value through profit or loss in accordance with IFRS 9.
                                                                                                                                      IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
              A joint operator accounts for the assets, liabilities, revenues and expenses relating to its interest in a joint
                                                                                                                                     IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2
              operation in accordance with the IFRS applicable to the particular asset, liability, revenue and expense.

              The acquisition of an interest in a joint operation in which the activity constitutes a business should be
                                                                                                                                                          83
              accounted for using the principles of IFRS 3.

IFRS 13      Fair Value Measurement                                                                                                        IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview     Defines fair value and provides guidance, how to estimate it and the required disclosures about fair value                    IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
             measurements.                                                                                                                 IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13

             IFRS 13 applies when another Standard requires or permits fair value measurements or disclosures about                        IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
             fair value measurements (and measurements such as fair value less costs to sell) but does not stipulate which                 IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
             items should be measured or disclosed at fair value.                                                                           IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
Fair value   Fair value is the price that would be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly transaction
                                                                                                                                           IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
             between market participants at the measurement date.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
             A fair value measurement assumes that the asset or liability is exchanged in an orderly transaction between market
                                                                                                                                           IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
             participants, under current market conditions.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                                                                                                                                           IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                                                                                                                                           IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                                                                                                                                           IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                          IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2



                                                                                                                                                               87
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                           Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                      Deloitte
                                                                                                                                       IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                            Accounting
                                                                                                                                       Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                       Standards,
                                                                                                                                         Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                       (DART)
                                                                                                                                                           Practice
                                                                                                                                       Statements
                                                                                                                                         Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Fair value   When an entity estimates fair value, the estimate is classified on the basis of the nature of the inputs the entity
hierarchy    has used.                                                                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
             Level 1 inputs are quoted prices in active markets for identical assets and liabilities that the entity can access
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
             at the measurement date.
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
             Level 2 inputs are those other than quoted market prices included within Level 1 that are observable for the
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
             asset or liability, either directly or indirectly. Level 2 inputs include quoted prices for similar assets and interest
             rates and yield curves observable at commonly quoted intervals.                                                              IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                                                                                                                                         IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
             Level 3 inputs are unobservable for the asset or liability. Examples include an entity using its own data to
             forecast the cash flows of a cash-generating unit (CGU) or estimating future volatility on the basis of historical          IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
             volatility.                                                                                                                 IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27

             Entities are required to use valuation techniques that maximise the use of relevant observable inputs                       IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
             and minimise the use of unobservable inputs. However, the objective of estimating the exit price at the                     IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
             measurement date remains the same regardless of the extent to which unobservable inputs are used.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
Disclosure   The disclosures depend on the nature of the fair value measurement (e.g. whether it is recognised in the
                                                                                                                                         IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
             financial statements or merely disclosed) and the level in which it is classified.
                                                                                                                                        IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2
             The disclosure requirements are most extensive when level 3 inputs are used, including sensitivity analysis.

IFRS 14            Regulatory Deferral Accounts                                                                                          IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview           The Standard permits an entity that adopts IFRS Standards after IFRS 14 was issued to continue to account,            IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                   with some limited changes, for ‘regulatory deferral account balances’ in accordance with its previous GAAP.           IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13

                   IFRS 14 was issued as a temporary solution pending a more comprehensive review of rate regulation by the              IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                   IASB.                                                                                                                 IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
Regulatory         Regulatory deferral account balances relate to the provision of goods or services to customers at a price or rate      IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
deferral account   that is subject to rate regulation.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
balances
                   Regulatory deferral account balances are presented separately in the statement of financial position and              IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                   movements in these account balances must also be presented separately in the statement of profit or loss and
                                                                                                                                         IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                   other comprehensive income. Specific disclosures are also required.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                   The requirements of other IFRS Standards are required to be applied to regulatory deferral account balances,          IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                   subject to specific exceptions, exemptions and additional requirements as noted in the Standard.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
Interpretations    None
                                                                                                                                         IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12

Pending changes In January 2021, the IASB published ED/2021/1 Regulatory Assets and Regulatory Liabilities proposing a new                IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                Standard that is intended to replace IFRS 14 by introducing a comprehensive accounting model for regulatory
                                                                                                                                          IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                assets and liabilities.
                                                                                                                                          IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                   The new Standard is proposed to apply when the entity is party to a regulatory agreement that determines                IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                   the regulated rate the entity can charge for the goods or services it supplies to customers.
                                                                                                                                          IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                   Regulatory assets and liabilities arise when part or all of the total allowed compensation for goods or services       IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                   supplied in one period is charged to customers through the regulated rates for goods or services supplied in
                                                                                                                                          IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                   a different past or future period. An entity recognises all regulatory assets and liabilities as defined under the
                   proposals, and as a result, regulatory income and expense.                                                             IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                                                                                                                                          IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                   Regulatory assets and liabilities would be measured at historical cost, modified for subsequent measurement
                   by using updated estimates of the amount and timing of future cash flows. The estimated future cash flows of           IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                   a regulatory asset or liability would be discounted to their present value by using the regulatory interest rate.      IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12

                   The final Standard is expected in 2025.                                                                               IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2
