IFRS 15           Revenue from Contracts with Customers                                                                                      IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview          Prescribes the accounting for revenue from sales of goods and rendering of services to a customer.                         IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                             IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                  The Standard applies only to revenue that arises from a contract with a customer. Other revenue such as from
                  dividends received would be recognised in accordance with other Standards.                                                 IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16

Contract with a   A contract with a customer is within the scope of this Standard when it has commercial substance, the parties have         IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
customer          approved it, the rights of the parties regarding the goods or services to be transferred and the payment terms can          IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                  be identified, the parties are committed to perform their obligations and enforce their rights and it is probable that
                                                                                                                                             IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                  the entity will collect the consideration to which it is entitled.
                                                                                                                                             IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
Core principle    The Standard uses a control model.
                                                                                                                                             IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                  An entity recognises revenue to depict the transfer of promised goods or services to customers in an amount
                                                                                                                                             IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                  that reflects the consideration to which the entity expects to be entitled in exchange for those goods or
                  services.                                                                                                                  IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                                                                                                                                             IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                                                                                                                                             IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                            IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2



                                                                                                                                                                 92
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                       Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                  Deloitte
                                                                                                                                   IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                        Accounting
                                                                                                                                   Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                   Standards,
                                                                                                                                     Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                   (DART)
                                                                                                                                                       Practice
                                                                                                                                   Statements
                                                                                                                                     Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Five steps   The Standard sets out five steps an entity applies to meet the core principle.
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
             Step 1: Identify the contract with a customer. It is the contract that creates enforceable rights and obligations       IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
             between the entity and its customer.
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
             Step 2: Identify the performance obligations in the contract. Each promise to transfer to a customer a good or          IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
             service that is distinct is a performance obligation and is accounted for separately.
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
             Step 3: Determine the transaction price. The transaction price is the amount of consideration to which the               IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
             entity expects to be entitled in exchange for transferring promised goods or services to the customer. It could
                                                                                                                                     IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
             be a fixed or variable amount or in a form other than cash. If the consideration is variable, the entity must
             estimate the amount to which it expects to be entitled, but recognises it only to the extent that it is highly          IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
             probable that a significant reversal will not occur when the uncertainty is resolved. The transaction price is          IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
             adjusted for the effects of the time value of money if the contract includes a significant financing component.
                                                                                                                                     IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
             Step 4: Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations in the contract. The transaction price is         IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
             allocated to each performance obligation on the basis of the relative stand-alone selling prices of each distinct
                                                                                                                                     IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
             good or service promised in the contract. If a stand-alone selling price is not observable, an entity estimates it.
                                                                                                                                     IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
             Step 5: Recognise revenue when (or as) the entity satisfies a performance obligation. Revenue is recognised            IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2
             when (or as) the performance obligation is satisfied and the customer obtains control of that good or service.
             This can be at a point in time (typically for goods) or over time (typically for services). The revenue recognised
             is the amount allocated to the satisfied performance obligation.                                                                            93
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                       Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                  Deloitte
                                                                                                                                   IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                        Accounting
                                                                                                                                   Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                   Standards,
                                                                                                                                     Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                   (DART)
                                                                                                                                                       Practice
                                                                                                                                   Statements
                                                                                                                                     Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Application        The Standard includes application guidance for specific transactions such as performance obligations
guidance           satisfied over time, methods for measuring progress of performance obligations, sales with a right of return,     IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                   warranties, principal versus agent considerations, customer options for additional goods or services, non-        IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                   refundable upfront fees, bill and hold arrangements and customers unexercised rights, licensing, repurchase       IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                   agreements, consignment arrangements and customer acceptance.
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                   The Standard also includes guidance on variable consideration and time value of money and specific                IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                   disclosure requirements.
                                                                                                                                      IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
Interpretations    None
                                                                                                                                     IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                                                                                                                                     IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
Changes            None                                                                                                              IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
effective this
                                                                                                                                     IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
year
                                                                                                                                     IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
Pending changes In June 2023, the IASB published Request for Information Post-implementation Review of IFRS 15 Revenue from
                Contracts with Customers to seek stakeholders’ feedback on its PIR of IFRS 15.                                       IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39

IFRS 16    Leases                                                                                                                         IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview   Sets out the recognition, measurement, presentation and disclosure requirements for leases.                                    IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                          IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
           A lessee recognises a leased asset and lease obligation for all leases. Lessors continue to distinguish between
           operating and finance leases.                                                                                                  IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16

Summary    A contract is, or contains, a lease if it conveys the right to control the use of an identified asset for a period of time     IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
           in exchange for consideration. Control is conveyed when the customer has the right to direct the identified asset’s             IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
           use and to obtain substantially its economic benefits from that use.
                                                                                                                                          IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                                                                                                                                          IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                                                                                                                                          IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                                                                                                                                          IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                                                                                                                                          IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                                                                                                                                          IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                                                                                                                                          IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12

                                                                                                                                            IFRS 1    IFRS 2     IFRS 3
Accounting by a   The Standard has a single lessee accounting model, requiring lessees to recognise a right-of-use asset and
lessee            a lease liability. The right-of-use asset is measured initially at the amount of the lease liability plus any initial     IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                  direct costs incurred by the lessee.                                                                                      IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                            IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                  After lease commencement, the right-of-use asset is accounted for in accordance with IAS 16 (unless specific
                  conditions apply).                                                                                                        IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                                                                                                                                            IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                  The lease liability is measured initially at the present value of the lease payments payable over the lease term,
                  discounted at the rate implicit in the lease if that can be readily determined. If that rate cannot be readily             IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                  determined, the lessee uses its incremental borrowing rate. Lease payments are allocated between interest                 IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                  expense and repayment of the lease liability.
                                                                                                                                            IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                  When the lease payments are variable the lessee does not include those when measuring the right-of-use                    IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                  asset and the lease liability, but instead recognises the amounts payable as they fall due. The exception is
                                                                                                                                            IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
                  variable payments that depend on an index or a rate, which are included in the initial measurement of a lease
                  liability and the right-of-use asset.                                                                                     IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                                                                                                                                            IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                  There are optional recognition exemptions when the lease term is 12 months or less or when the underlying
                  asset has a low value when new. If applied, the lease payments are recognised on a basis that represents the              IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                  pattern of the lessee’s benefit (e.g. straight-line over the lease term).                                                IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2

Accounting by a   The IFRS 16 approach to lessor accounting is substantially unchanged from its predecessor, IAS 17.
lessor                                                                                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                  Lessors classify each lease as an operating lease or a finance lease.                                                  IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                  A lease is classified as a finance lease if it transfers substantially all the risks and rewards incidental to         IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                  ownership of an underlying asset. Otherwise a lease is classified as an operating lease.                               IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16

                  A lessor recognises assets held under a finance lease as a receivable at an amount equal to the net                    IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
                  investment in the lease upon lease commencement.                                                                        IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10

                  For sale and leaseback transactions, the seller is required to determine whether the transfer of an asset is           IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                  a sale by applying the requirements of IFRS 15. If it is a sale the seller measures the right-of-use asset at the      IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                  proportion of the previous carrying amount that relates to the right of use retained. As a result, the seller only
                                                                                                                                         IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
                  recognises the amount of gain or loss that relates to the rights transferred to the buyer.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
Interpretations   None

Pending changes In September 2022, the IASB published Lease Liability in a Sale and Leaseback (Amendments to IFRS 16). The
                amendments require a seller-lessee to measure the lease liability arising from a leaseback in a way that it does     IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7
                not result in recognition of a gain or loss that relates to the right of use it retains, after the commencement      IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                date. The amendments are effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after 1 January 2024 with            IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
                earlier application permitted.
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
                    In the second half of 2023, the IASB will consider when to begin the PIR of IFRS 16.                             IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2

                    In addition, the IASB has added a project on Sale and Leaseback of an Asset in a Single-Asset Entity to its       IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                    maintenance project pipeline. If undertaken, this narrow-scope project would address how an entity accounts      IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                    for the sale of a subsidiary when the entity leases back one or more of the assets held by the subsidiary.

IFRS 17         Insurance Contracts                                                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6     IFRS 7

Overview        Establishes the principles for the recognition, measurement, presentation and disclosure of insurance                 IFRS 8    IFRS 9    IFRS 10
                contracts.                                                                                                            IFRS 11   IFRS 12   IFRS 13
Insurance and   IFRS 17 specifies how an entity recognises, measures, presents and discloses insurance contracts, reinsurance         IFRS 14   IFRS 15   IFRS 16
reinsurance     contracts and investment contracts with discretionary participation features.
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 17    IAS 1     IAS 2
contracts
                An insurance contract is one in which the issuer accepts significant insurance risk by agreeing to compensate the      IAS 7     IAS 8     IAS 10
                policyholder for the insured event.
                                                                                                                                      IAS 12    IAS 16     IAS 19
                A reinsurance contract is an insurance contract issued by the reinsurer to compensate another entity for claims       IAS 20    IAS 21     IAS 23
                arising from one or more insurance contracts it holds as an issuer.
                                                                                                                                      IAS 24    IAS 26     IAS 27
Aggregation     Entities must identify portfolios of insurance contracts, being those contracts that have similar risks and are
                                                                                                                                      IAS 28    IAS 29     IAS 32
of insurance    managed together, such as within a product line.
contracts                                                                                                                             IAS 33    IAS 34     IAS 36
                Each portfolio is divided into groups of insurance contracts on the basis of, at a minimum, those that at initial
                                                                                                                                      IAS 37    IAS 38     IAS 39
                recognition are onerous, have no significant possibility of becoming onerous subsequently or do not fall into
                either category.                                                                                                      IAS 40    IAS 41    IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                     IFRIC 17    PS 1          PS 2
                Groups of insurance contracts cannot include contracts issued more than one year apart.

IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                       Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                  Deloitte

                                                                                                                                     Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Recognition   A group of insurance contracts is recognised from the earlier of the beginning of its coverage period or
              the date when the first payment from a policyholder in the group becomes due, or for a group of onerous                IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
              contracts, when the group becomes onerous.                                                                             IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
Initial       On initial recognition, an entity measures a group of insurance contracts at the total of the group’s fulfilment       IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
measurement   cash flows (FCF) and the contractual service margin (CSM).
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
              The FCF comprises an estimate of future cash flows, an adjustment to reflect the time value of money and the           IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
              financial risks associated with the future cash flows and a risk adjustment for non-financial risk.                     IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
              The CSM is the unearned profit of the group of insurance contracts that the entity will recognise as it provides       IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
              services in the future. The CSM of a group of onerous contracts is nil and the group’s measurement consists            IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
              entirely of fulfilment cash flows. The net outflow expected from a group of contracts determined to be
                                                                                                                                     IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
              onerous on initial recognition is recognised at that date in profit or loss.
                                                                                                                                     IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
              For profitable contracts, the CSM is measured on initial recognition at an amount that results in no income or
                                                                                                                                     IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
              expenses arising from the initial recognition of the FCF, the derecognition at that date of any asset or liability
              recognised for insurance acquisition cash flows and any cash flows arising from the contracts in the group at          IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
              that date.                                                                                                             IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                    IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2

                                                                                                                                IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
        IFRS 17 also requires an entity to include in the initial measurement of the CSM of a group of insurance
        contracts the effect of the derecognition of any asset or liability previously recognised for cash flows related to     IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
        that group paid or received before the group is recognised. This also applies to assets and liabilities previously      IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
        recognised because of the requirements of another IFRS Standard even if no cash flows have been paid or                 IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
        received.
                                                                                                                                IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
        An entity is required to use a systematic and rational method to allocate the insurance acquisition cash flows          IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
        that are directly attributable to a group of insurance contracts to that group and to groups that will include
                                                                                                                                 IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
        insurance contracts that are expected to arise from renewals of the insurance contracts in that group.
                                                                                                                                IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
        Insurance acquisition cash flows that are directly attributable to a portfolio of insurance contracts, but that
                                                                                                                                IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
        are not directly attributable to individual contracts or groups of contracts, are allocated based on a systematic
        and rational method to groups in the portfolio.                                                                         IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                                                                                                                                IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
        An entity recognises those cash flows as an asset until the groups of related contract renewals or groups
        expected to be in the portfolio are recognised.                                                                         IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36

Subsequent    The carrying amount of a group of insurance contracts at the end of each reporting period is the sum of the
measurement   liability for remaining coverage (comprising the FCF related to future services and the CSM at that date) and        IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
              the liability for incurred claims.                                                                                   IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                   IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
              The CSM is adjusted at the end of each reporting period to reflect the change in fulfilment cash flows on a
              group of insurance contracts that relates to the future service to be provided. Revenue is comprised of the          IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
              amount of premium that compensates for insurance service expense (as expected at the beginning of the                IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
              reporting period) and the release of CSM based on the amount of service provided in the period expressed
                                                                                                                                    IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
              in coverage units. The CSM is allocated to coverage units considering the quantities of benefits and expected
              period of both insurance coverage and any investment-return or investment-related service. The CSM is                IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
              released in full over the coverage period.                                                                           IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

              Direct participating contracts are contracts that create an obligation for the entity to pay to the policyholder     IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
              an amount equal to the underlying items less a variable fee. The variable fee comprises the entity’s share of        IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
              the fair value of the underlying items less amounts payable to the policyholder that do not vary based on
                                                                                                                                   IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
              the underlying items. The general measurement model is modified for such contracts. This modification is
              referred to as the Variable Fee Approach.                                                                            IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39

              For groups of contracts with a coverage period of less than one year, or where it is reasonably expected to          IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
              produce a liability measurement that would not differ materially from the general approach under IFRS 17, a         IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
              simplified Premium Allocation Approach can be applied.

              Specific measurement requirements apply to onerous insurance contracts, reinsurance contracts and
                                                                                                                                                       102
              investment contracts with discretionary participation features.

IAS 1          Presentation of Financial Statements                                                                                  IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview       Sets out the overall framework for presenting general purpose financial statements, including guidelines for          IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
               their structure and the minimum content.                                                                              IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Complete set   A complete set of financial statements comprises:                                                                     IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
of financial
               •   A statement of financial position                                                                                 IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
statements
               •   A statement of profit or loss and other comprehensive income                                                       IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
               •   A statement of changes in equity
                                                                                                                                     IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
               •   A statement of cash flows
               •   Notes                                                                                                             IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                                                                                                                                     IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
               Entities may use titles for the individual financial statements other than those used above.
                                                                                                                                     IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
               Comparative information for the prior period is required for amounts shown in the financial statements and the        IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
               notes.
                                                                                                                                     IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
               Financial statements are generally prepared annually. If the end of the reporting period changes, and financial       IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
               statements are presented for a period other than one year, additional disclosures are required.
                                                                                                                                    IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
               A third statement of financial position is required when an accounting policy has been applied retrospectively or
               items in the financial statements have been restated or reclassified.

Materiality          IAS 1 defines what makes information material to the primary users of the financial statements. It also sets
                     out the line items to be presented in each of the statements (with the exception of the statement of cash              IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                     flows, for which IAS 7 sets out the requirements) and has guidance for when an entity presents additional line         IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                     items or subtotals.                                                                                                    IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                     IFRS Practice Statement 2 Making Materiality Judgements provides guidance on making materiality judgements             IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                     when preparing general purpose financial statements in accordance with IFRS Standards.                                 IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
Statement of         In the statement of financial position, assets and liabilities are required to be classified as current or non-         IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
financial position   current, unless presenting them in order of liquidity provides reliable and more relevant information. Assets
                     and liabilities may not be offset unless offsetting is permitted or required by another IFRS Standard.                 IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19

Statement of         The statement of profit or loss and other comprehensive income includes all items of income and expense.               IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
profit or loss       It can be presented as either a single statement, with a subtotal for profit or loss, or as separate statements        IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
and other            of profit or loss and other comprehensive income. Within the profit or loss section expenses are presented
                                                                                                                                            IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
comprehensive        either by their nature (e.g. depreciation) or by function (e.g. cost of sales). If they are presented by function,
income               additional disclosures about their nature are required to be presented in the notes. Items can only be                 IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                     presented in other comprehensive income if permitted by an IFRS Standard and are grouped based on                      IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                     whether or not they are potentially reclassifiable to profit or loss at a later date. Income and expenses may not      IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                     be offset unless offsetting is permitted or required by another IFRS Standard.
                                                                                                                                           IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                     There are special presentation requirements for discontinued activities and assets held for sale—see IFRS 5.

Statement of        The statement of changes in equity is required to show the total comprehensive income for the period; the
changes in equity   effects on each component of equity of retrospective application or retrospective restatement in accordance          IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                    with IAS 8; and for each component of equity, a reconciliation between the opening and closing balances,             IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10

                                                                                                                                         IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                    has made in the process of applying the entity’s accounting policies that have the most significant effect on
                    the amounts recognised in the financial statements; sources of estimation uncertainty; and management of             IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                    capital and compliance with capital requirements.                                                                     IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
Fundamental         IAS 1 also sets out the fundamental principles for the preparation of financial statements, including the going      IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19

Changes             The IASB published amendments to IAS 1 and IFRS Practice Statement 2 titled Disclosure of Accounting Policies.       IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36

year                prospectively. Earlier application is permitted.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                    Applying the amendments, an entity discloses its material accounting policies, instead of its significant           IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                    accounting policies. The revised Standard also explains how an entity can identify a material accounting policy.
                    Examples of when an accounting policy is likely to be material were added.

Pending changes The IASB published amendments to IAS 1 titled Classification of Liabilities which are effective for annual
                periods beginning on or after 1 January 2024 with earlier application permitted. An entity should apply those                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                amendments retrospectively.                                                                                                     IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                                IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                    The amendments specify that the classification of liabilities as current or non-current is based on rights that are
                    in existence at the end of the reporting period. The classification is unaffected by expectations about whether             IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                    an entity will exercise its right to defer settlement of a liability. Rights are in existence if covenants are complied     IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                    with at the end of the reporting period. The amendments also introduce a definition of ‘settlement’ to make clear
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                    that settlement refers to the transfer to the counterparty of cash, equity instruments, other assets or services.
                                                                                                                                                IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                    The IASB also published Non-current Liabilities with Covenants, which amends IAS 1 to specify that conditions
                                                                                                                                                IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                    an entity must comply with in the twelve months after the reporting period do not affect classification of the
                    corresponding liability as current or non-current. The amendments also require additional disclosure on the                 IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                    conditions an entity is required to comply with after the reporting period. The amendments are effective for                IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                    annual periods beginning on or after 1 January 2024 with earlier application permitted. An entity should apply
                                                                                                                                                IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                    those amendments retrospectively.
                                                                                                                                                IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                    In addition, the IASB proposed a new IFRS Standard titled General Presentation and Disclosures in ED/2019/7.
                                                                                                                                                IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                    The new Standard, if finalised, would replace IAS 1, carrying forward many of the requirements in IAS 1                    IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                    unchanged and complementing them with new requirements.

IAS 2                Inventories                                                                                                                 IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview                                                                                                                                         IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                     Prescribes the accounting for inventories.
                                                                                                                                                 IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Initial              Inventories are stated at the lower of cost and net realisable value (NRV).
measurement of                                                                                                                                   IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
inventory            Costs include purchase cost, conversion cost (materials, labour and overheads), and other costs to bring inventory
                                                                                                                                                 IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                     to its present location and condition, but not foreign exchange differences (see IAS 21).
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                     For inventory that is not interchangeable, specific costs are attributed to the specific individual items of inventory.
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                     For interchangeable items, cost is determined on either a first in first out (FIFO) or weighted average basis. Last in
                     first out (LIFO) is not permitted.                                                                                          IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

Cost of goods sold   When inventory is sold, the carrying amount is recognised as an expense in the period in which the related                  IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                     revenue is recognised.                                                                                                      IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
Impairment           Write-downs to NRV are recognised as an expense in the period the loss occurs. Reversals arising from an                    IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                     increase in NRV are recognised as a reduction of the inventory expense in the period in which they occur.
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
Interpretations      None
                                                                                                                                                 IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12

IAS 7               Statement of Cash Flows                                                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview            Requires a statement of cash flows to present information about changes in cash and cash equivalents,                     IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                    classified as operating, investing and financing activities.                                                              IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Cash and cash       Cash equivalents include investments that are short-term (less than three months from date of acquisition), readily       IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
equivalents         convertible to a known amount of cash, and subject to an insignificant risk of changes in value.
                                                                                                                                              IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
Operating,          Operating activities are the principal revenue-producing activities of the entity and other activities that are
                                                                                                                                               IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
investing and       not investing or financing activities. Operating cash flows are reported using either the direct (recommended)
financing cash      or the indirect method. Cash flows from taxes on income are classified as operating unless they can be                    IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
flows               specifically identified with financing or investing activities. Investing activities are the acquisition and disposal     IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                    of long-term assets and other investments not included in cash equivalents. Financing activities are activities
                                                                                                                                              IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                    that result in changes in the size and composition of the contributed equity and borrowings of the entity.
                                                                                                                                              IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                    Aggregate cash flows from obtaining or losing control of subsidiaries are presented separately and classified
                                                                                                                                              IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                    as investing activities.
                                                                                                                                              IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                    Investing and financing transactions that do not require the use of cash are excluded from the statement of               IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                    cash flows but need to be disclosed.
                                                                                                                                             IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
Reconciliation of  Entities must reconcile the opening and closing amounts in the statement of financial position for items
financing balances classified as financing activities.

                amendments to IAS 7 require entities to provide qualitative and quantitative information about its supplier                IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

                    In addition, the IASB proposed a new IFRS Standard titled General Presentation and Disclosures in ED/2019/7            IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                    that would also make consequential amendments to IAS 7.                                                                IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2

                    The ED proposes that entities would no longer have a choice as to where to present cash flows from dividends            IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                    and interest. For most entities, dividends and interest paid would be cash flows from financing activities, while      IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                    dividends and interest received would be cash flows from investing activities. It would also require that the
                                                                                                                                           IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

                    would address the potential confusion in applying IAS 7:37 arising from the use of the term ‘cost method’ that         IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                    is no longer defined in IFRS Accounting Standards.

                    comprehensively or make more targeted improvements.

IAS 8        Accounting Policies, Changes in Accounting Estimates and Errors                                                       IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview     Prescribes the criteria for selecting and changing accounting policies, together with the accounting treatment        IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
             and disclosure of changes in accounting policies, changes in estimates and correction of errors.                      IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Selecting    Entities must apply the Standards and Interpretations issued by the IASB. In the absence of a directly applicable     IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
accounting   IFRS Standard, entities must look to the requirements in IFRS Standards that address similar and related issues
                                                                                                                                   IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
policies     and, failing that, to the Conceptual Framework. Entities may also consider the most recent pronouncements of
             other standard-setting bodies that use a similar conceptual framework, other accounting literature and accepted        IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
             industry practice.                                                                                                    IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
Changes in   Accounting policies must be applied consistently to similar transactions. Voluntary changes can be made only          IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
accounting   if the change results in reliable and more relevant information.
                                                                                                                                   IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
policies
             When a change in accounting policy is required by an IFRS Standard, the pronouncement’s transitional                  IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
             requirements are followed. If the new requirement is not yet mandatory, and the entity has not early-applied
                                                                                                                                   IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
             the change, the entity must provide information it knows, or can reasonably estimate, about the possible
             effect that application will have on its financial statements when it plans to apply the new requirements.            IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                                                                                                                                   IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
             If the entity makes a change voluntarily, the new policy must be applied retrospectively and prior periods are
             restated. The Standard provides relief from retrospective application when it is impracticable to determine          IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
             period-specific effects.

                                                                                                                                         IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Changes in          Changes in accounting estimates (e.g. change in useful life of an asset) are accounted for prospectively, in the
accounting          current year, or future years, or both. The comparative information is not restated.                                 IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
estimates                                                                                                                                IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
Prior period errors All material prior period errors are corrected by restating comparative prior period amounts and, if the error       IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                    occurred before the earliest period presented, by restating the opening statement of financial position.
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
Interpretations     None                                                                                                                                       IAS 2
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 17    IAS 1
                                                                                                                                          IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10

Changes             The IASB published amendments to IAS 8 titled Definition of Accounting Estimates that are effective for              IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
effective this      annual reporting periods beginning on or after 1 January 2023 in respect of changes in accounting policies           IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
year                and changes in accounting estimates that occur on or after the start of that period. Earlier application is
                                                                                                                                         IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                    permitted.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                    The amendments replace the definition of a change in accounting estimates with a definition of accounting            IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                    estimates. Under the new definition, accounting estimates are “monetary amounts in financial statements
                                                                                                                                         IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                    that are subject to measurement uncertainty”.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                    The revised Standard clarifies that a change in accounting estimate that results from new information or
                                                                                                                                        IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                    new developments is not the correction of an error. In addition, the effects of a change in an input or a
                    measurement technique used to develop an accounting estimate are changes in accounting estimates if they
                    do not result from the correction of prior period errors.

IAS 10             Events after the Reporting Period                                                                                     IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview           Prescribes when financial statements must be adjusted for events after the end of the reporting period and            IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                   what information must be disclosed.                                                                                   IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Events after       Events after the end of the reporting period are those that occur between the end of the reporting period and the     IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
the end of the     date when the financial statements are authorised for issue.
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
reporting period
                                                                                                                                          IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
Adjusting events   The financial statements are adjusted for events that provide evidence of conditions that existed at the end of
                   the reporting period (such as the resolution of a court case after the end of the reporting period).                  IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                                                                                                                                         IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
Non-adjusting      The financial statements are not adjusted for events that arose after the end of the reporting period (such as
events             a decline in market prices after year end). The nature and effect of such events are disclosed. However, if the       IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                   events after the end of the reporting period indicate that the going concern assumption is not appropriate,           IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                   those financial statements are not prepared on a going concern basis.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                   Dividends proposed or declared after the end of the reporting period are not recognised as a liability at the         IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                   end of the reporting period.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
Interpretations    None
                                                                                                                                        IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2

                                                                                                                                         IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
IAS 12         Income Taxes                                                                                                              IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview                                                                                                                                 IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
               Sets out the accounting for current and deferred tax.
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Current and    Current tax liabilities and assets are recognised for current and prior period taxes, measured at the rates that have
deferred tax   been enacted or substantively enacted by the end of the reporting period.                                                 IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
               Deferred tax assets and liabilities are the income taxes recoverable or payable in future periods as a result of
               differences between the amounts attributed to assets and liabilities from applying IFRS Standards and the                  IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
               amounts those assets and liabilities are attributed for tax purposes (called temporary differences).                      IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
Deferred tax   Deferred tax liabilities are recognised for the future tax consequences of all taxable temporary differences              IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
liabilities    with three exceptions:
                                                                                                                                         IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
               A deferred tax liability is not recognised when the temporary difference arises from the initial recognition              IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
               of goodwill; when, at the time of the transaction, the initial recognition of an asset or liability does not affect
                                                                                                                                         IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
               either the accounting or the taxable profit (unless it is a business combination); and for differences arising
               from investments in subsidiaries, branches, associates and joint arrangements (e.g. due to undistributed                  IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
               profits) when the entity is able to control the timing of the reversal of the difference and it is probable that the      IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
               reversal will not occur in the foreseeable future.                                                                       IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2



                                                                                                                                                             122
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                         Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                    Deloitte
                                                                                                                                     IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                          Accounting Standards,
                                                                                                                                       Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                     Interpretations
                                                                                                                                        IFRS 1
                                                                                                                                                     (DART)
                                                                                                                                                         Practice
                                                                                                                                     Statements
                                                                                                                                       Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Deferred tax     A deferred tax asset is recognised for deductible temporary differences, unused tax losses and unused
assets           tax credits, but only to the extent that it is probable that taxable profit will be available against which the       IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                 deductible temporary differences can be utilised.                                                                     IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                 There are two exceptions: a deferred tax asset is not recognised for temporary differences related to the
                 initial recognition of an asset or liability, other than in a business combination, which, at the time of the         IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                 transaction, does not affect the accounting or the taxable profit; and deferred tax assets arising from               IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                 deductible temporary differences associated with investments in subsidiaries, branches and associates,
                                                                                                                                        IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                 and interests in joint arrangements are recognised only to the extent that it is probable that the temporary
                 difference will reverse in the foreseeable future and taxable profit will be available to utilise the difference.     IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                                                                                                                                       IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                 A reassessment of unrecognised deferred tax assets must be made at the end of each reporting period.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
Measurement of   Deferred tax liabilities and assets are measured at the tax rates expected to apply when the liability is settled
deferred tax     or the asset is realised, based on tax rates or laws that have been enacted or substantively enacted by the           IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                 end of the reporting period. Deferred tax assets and liabilities are not discounted.                                  IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36

                 The measurement must reflect the tax consequences that would follow from the manner in which the entity               IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                 expects to recover or settle the carrying amount of its assets and liabilities. There is a rebuttable presumption     IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                 that recovery of the carrying amount of an investment property measured at fair value will be through sale.
                                                                                                                                      IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2

                                                                                                                                           IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Presentation      Current and deferred tax is recognised as income or expense in profit or loss unless it relates to a transaction
of current and    or event that is recognised outside profit or loss or to a business combination.                                         IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
deferred tax                                                                                                                               IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                  Deferred tax assets and liabilities are classified as non-current items.
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Interpretations   SIC-25 Income Taxes—Changes in the Tax Status of an Entity or its Shareholders clarifies that the current and
                  deferred tax consequences of changes in tax status are included in profit or loss even when they relate to               IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                  transactions or events that were previously recognised outside profit or loss.                                           IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                                                                                                                                            IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                  IFRIC 23 Uncertainty over Income Tax Treatments clarifies that entities must assess whether it is probable that a
                  tax authority (with full knowledge of all relevant information) will accept an uncertain tax treatment used in tax       IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                  filings. If so, tax accounting should be consistent with that treatment. If not, the effect of uncertainty should be     IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                  reflected in the tax accounting applied (using whichever of a ‘most likely amount’ or ‘expected value’ approach
                                                                                                                                           IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                  is expected to better predict the resolution of the uncertainty).
                                                                                                                                           IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32

Changes          In May 2021, the IASB published Deferred Tax related to Assets and Liabilities arising from a Single Transaction
effective this   (Amendments to IAS 12). The amendments introduce an exception to the initial recognition exemption in                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
year             IAS 12. Applying this exception, an entity does not apply the initial recognition exemption to transactions             IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                 that give rise to equal taxable and deductible temporary differences. The amendments apply to transactions              IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                 that occur on or after the beginning of the earliest comparative period presented. They also apply to
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                 taxable and deductible temporary differences associated with right-of-use assets and lease liabilities, and
                 decommissioning obligations and corresponding amounts recognised as assets at the beginning of the                      IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                 earliest comparative period presented. The amendments are effective for annual reporting periods beginning               IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                 on or after 1 January 2023. Early application of the amendments is permitted.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                 In May 2023, the IASB published International Tax Reform—Pillar Two Model Rules (Amendments to IAS 12).                 IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                 The amendments introduce a temporary exception from accounting for deferred taxes arising from the
                                                                                                                                         IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                 implementation of the OECD Pillar Two model rules, together with targeted disclosure requirements for
                 affected entities.                                                                                                      IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                                                                                                                                         IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                 Applying the exception, an entity does not recognise deferred tax assets and liabilities related to the OECD
                 Pillar Two income taxes. It also does not disclose any information about these deferred tax assets and                  IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                 liabilities. In periods in which Pillar Two legislation is enacted or substantively enacted, but not yet in effect,     IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                 an entity is required to disclose known or reasonably estimable information that helps users of financial              IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                 statements understand the entity’s exposure to Pillar Two income taxes arising from that legislation.
