IAS 16               Property, Plant and Equipment                                                                                                IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview                                                                                                                                          IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                     Sets out the principles for accounting for property, plant and equipment (PP&E).
                                                                                                                                                  IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Initial recognition PP&E is recognised as an asset when it is probable that its future economic benefits will flow to the entity, and its
and measurement cost can be measured reliably. This includes bearer plants used in the production or supply of agricultural produce.              IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                                                                                                                                                  IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                     Initial recognition is at cost, which includes all costs necessary to get the asset ready for its intended use. Interest
                     on amounts borrowed for the purposes of constructing an asset are included in its cost—see IAS 23.                            IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                     Exchanges of PP&E are measured at fair value, including exchanges of similar items, unless the exchange
                     transaction lacks commercial substance or the fair value of neither the asset received nor the asset given up can            IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                     be measured reliably.                                                                                                        IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
Subsequent           After initial recognition PP&E is either carried at cost less accumulated depreciation and impairment (the cost              IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
measurement          model) or measured at fair value less accumulated depreciation and impairment between revaluations (the
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                     revaluation model). Any revaluation surplus on disposal of an asset remains in equity and is not reclassified to
                     profit or loss.                                                                                                              IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                     Impairment of PP&E is assessed under IAS 36.
                                                                                                                                                 IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2



                                                                                                                                                                      127
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                              Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                         Deloitte
                                                                                                                                          IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                               Accounting
                                                                                                                                          Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                          Standards,
                                                                                                                                            Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                          (DART)
                                                                                                                                                              Practice
                                                                                                                                          Statements
                                                                                                                                            Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                            IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Depreciation        Depreciation is charged systematically over the useful life of the asset, using a method that reflects the
                    pattern of benefit consumption, to its residual value. Different depreciation methods are acceptable (including         IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                    straight-line, diminishing balance and units of production), but not a method that is based on the revenue the          IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                    asset generates.                                                                                                        IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                    Components of an asset with differing patterns of benefits are depreciated separately.                                  IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                                                                                                                                            IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                    The residual value is the amount the entity would receive currently if the asset were already of the age and
                    condition expected at the end of its useful life. Useful life and the residual value are reviewed annually.              IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
Major inspections   If operation of an item of PP&E (e.g. an aircraft) requires regular major inspections, the cost of each major           IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                    inspection is recognised in the carrying amount of the asset, if the recognition criteria are satisfied.                IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
Previously rented   Entities that routinely sell items of PP&E that they have previously held to rent must transfer the PP&E to             IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
PP&E                inventory, at its carrying amount, when it becomes held for sale. The proceeds from the sale of such assets             IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                    are recognised in accordance with IFRS 15.
                                                                                                                                            IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
Interpretations     IFRIC 1 Changes in Existing Decommissioning, Restoration and Similar Liabilities clarifies that the carrying amount
                    of an asset is adjusted when there is a change in the estimated decommissioning or restoration liability                IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                    related to that asset.                                                                                                  IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                           IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                    IFRIC 20 Stripping Costs in the Production Phase of a Surface Mine addresses recognition of production stripping
                    costs and measurement (initial and subsequent) of that stripping activity asset.

IAS 19               Employee Benefits                                                                                                      IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview             Sets out the accounting and disclosure requirements for employee benefits, including short-term benefits               IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                     (wages, annual leave, sick leave, annual profit-sharing, bonuses and non-monetary benefits), pensions,                 IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                     post-employment life insurance and medical benefits, other long-term employee benefits (long-service leave,
                                                                                                                                            IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                     disability, deferred compensation, and long-term profit-sharing and bonuses); and termination benefits.
                                                                                                                                            IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
Basic principle      The cost of providing employee benefits is recognised in the period in which the entity receives services from the
                     employee, rather than when the benefits are paid or payable.                                                            IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                                                                                                                                            IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                     Short-term employee benefits (expected to be settled wholly before 12 months after the annual period in which
                     the services were rendered) are recognised as an expense in the period in which the employee renders the               IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                     service. Unpaid benefit liability is measured at an undiscounted amount.                                               IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27

                     Profit-sharing and bonus payments are recognised only when the entity has a legal or constructive obligation to        IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                     pay them and the costs can be estimated reliably.                                                                      IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
Post-employment      Post-employment benefit plans (such as pensions and health care) are categorised as either defined                     IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
benefits             contribution plans or defined benefit plans.
                                                                                                                                            IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
Defined              Expenses are recognised in the period in which the contribution is payable.                                           IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
contribution plans

IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                               Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                          Deloitte
                                                                                                                                           IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                                Accounting
                                                                                                                                           Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                           Standards,

                                                                                                                                                               Practice
                                                                                                                                           Statements
                                                                                                                                             Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                             IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Defined benefit   A liability (or asset) is recognised equal to the net of the present value of the obligations under the defined
plans             benefit plan and the fair value of the plan assets at the end of the reporting period. The present value is                IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                  calculated using a rate determined with reference to market yields on high-quality corporate bonds.                        IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                             IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                  Plan assets include assets held by a long-term employee benefit fund and qualifying insurance policies.
                                                                                                                                             IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                  A defined benefit asset is limited to the lower of the surplus in the defined benefit plan and present value of
                                                                                                                                             IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                  any economic benefits available in the form of refunds from the plan or reductions in future contributions to
                  the plan.                                                                                                                   IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                                                                                                                                             IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                  The change in the defined benefit liability or asset is separated into the service cost, net interest and
                  remeasurements.                                                                                                            IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                                                                                                                                             IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                  The service cost is the increase in the present value of the defined benefit obligation resulting from the
                  service of employees in the current period and any change in the present value related to employee service in              IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                  prior periods that results from plan amendments. The service cost is recognised in profit or loss.                         IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36

                  Net interest is the change in the liability (asset) caused by the passage of time and is recognised in profit or loss.     IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                                                                                                                                             IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                  Remeasurements include actuarial gains or losses (such as changes in actuarial assumptions) and the return
                                                                                                                                            IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                  on plan assets and are recognised in OCI.

                  For group plans, the net cost is recognised in the separate financial statements of the entity that is legally the
                  sponsoring employer unless a contractual agreement or stated policy for allocating the cost exists.                                            131

Other long-term   Other long-term employee benefits are recognised and measured in the same way as post-employment
benefits          benefits under a defined benefit plan. However, unlike defined benefit plans, remeasurements are recognised      IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                  immediately in profit or loss.                                                                                   IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                                                                                                                                   IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                  Termination benefits are recognised at the earlier of when the entity can no longer withdraw the offer of
                  the benefits and when the entity recognises costs for a restructuring that is within the scope of IAS 37 and     IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                  involves the payment of termination benefits.                                                                    IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
Interpretations   IFRIC 14 IAS 19—The Limit on a Defined Benefit Asset, Minimum Funding Requirements and their Interaction          IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                  addresses when refunds or reductions in future contributions should be regarded as being ‘available’, how a
                  minimum funding requirement might affect the availability of reductions in future contributions and when a       IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                  minimum funding requirement might give rise to a liability.                                                      IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

                                                                                                                                         IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
IAS 20           Accounting for Government Grants and Disclosure of Government Assistance                                                IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview         Prescribes the accounting for, and disclosure of, government grants and other forms of government                       IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                 assistance.                                                                                                             IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Recognition of   A government grant is recognised only when there is reasonable assurance that the entity will comply with the           IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
Government       conditions attached to the grant and it will be received. Non-monetary grants are usually recognised at fair value,
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
Grants           although recognition at nominal value is permitted.
                                                                                                                                          IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                 The benefit of government loans with a below-market rate of interest is a government grant—measured as the
                                                                                                                                         IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                 difference between the initial carrying amount of the loan determined in accordance with IFRS 9 and the proceeds
                 received.                                                                                                               IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

Presentation     Grants are recognised in profit or loss over the periods necessary to match them with the related costs.                IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                                                                                                                                         IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                 Income-related grants are either presented separately as income or as a reduction of the related expense.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                 Asset-related grants are either presented as deferred income in the statement of financial position, or
                                                                                                                                         IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39

IAS 21               The Effects of Changes in Foreign Exchange Rates                                                                      IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview                                                                                                                                   IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                     Prescribes the accounting for foreign currency transactions and foreign operations.
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Functional           An entity’s functional currency is the currency of the primary economic environment in which the entity operates.
currency             All foreign currency items are translated into that currency.                                                         IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16

Exchange             Transactions are recognised on the date that they occur using the exchange rate on that date for initial              IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
differences on       recognition and measurement.                                                                                           IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
transactions                                                                                                                               IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
Exchange             At the end of a reporting period non-monetary items carried at historical amounts continue to be measured
                                                                                                                                           IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
differences on       using transaction-date exchange rates, monetary items are retranslated using the closing rate and non-
translation at the   monetary items carried at fair value are measured at valuation-date exchange rates.                                   IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
end of a reporting                                                                                                                         IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
period               Exchange differences arising on settlement or translation of monetary items are included in profit or loss,
                     with one exception. Exchange differences arising on monetary items that are part of the reporting entity’s net        IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                     investment in a foreign operation are recognised in the consolidated financial statements that include the            IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                     foreign operation in other comprehensive income. Such differences are reclassified from equity to profit or           IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                     loss on disposal of the net investment.

currency           of financial position presented (including comparatives) are translated at the closing rate at the date of each

Pending changes In August 2023, the IASB published amendments to IAS 21 titled Lack of Exchangeability that specify how                IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                to assess whether a currency is exchangeable and how to determine the exchange rate when it is not.
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                When a currency is not exchangeable at the measurement date, an entity is required to estimate the spot
                exchange rate as the rate that would have applied to an orderly exchange transaction at the measurement                IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                date between market participants under prevailing economic conditions. In that case, an entity is required              IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                to disclose information that enables users of its financial statements to evaluate how the currency’s lack of          IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                exchangeability affects, or is expected to affect, the entity’s financial performance, financial position and cash
                flows. Entities are required to apply the amendments for annual reporting periods beginning on or after 1              IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                January 2025 with earlier application permitted.                                                                       IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27

                    to the IASB work plan to address the translation method applied to the consolidation of a subsidiary, whose        IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                    functional currency is the currency of a non-hyperinflationary economy, by a parent, whose functional and
                                                                                                                                       IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                    presentation currency is the currency of a hyperinflationary economy, and by an entity whose functional
                    currency is the currency of a non-hyperinflationary economy but that presents its financial statements in the     IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                    currency of a hyperinflationary economy.

Recognition of    Borrowing costs directly attributable to the acquisition or construction of a qualifying asset are included in the cost
borrowing costs   of that asset. All other borrowing costs are expensed when incurred.                                                        IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
as a cost of                                                                                                                                  IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
construction      A qualifying asset is one that takes a substantial period of time to make it ready for its intended use or sale.

                  If funds are borrowed generally and used for the purpose of obtaining a qualifying asset, a capitalisation rate (using
                                                                                                                                              IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19

                  cannot exceed the amount of borrowing costs incurred.                                                                       IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

IAS 24          Related Party Disclosures                                                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview        Sets out disclosure requirements to make investors aware that the financial position and results of operations              IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                may have been affected by the existence of related parties.                                                                 IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Related party   A related party is a person or entity that is related to the reporting entity.                                              IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16

                A related party includes a person who has, or has a close family member who has, control or joint control of, or            IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                significant influence over, the reporting entity or is a member of its, or its parent’s, key management personnel.           IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                Entities that such a person controls, jointly controls, has significant influence over or of which they are a member of
                                                                                                                                            IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                the key management personnel are also related parties.
                                                                                                                                            IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                Another entity is related to the reporting entity if it is a member of the same group; either entity is an associate or
                                                                                                                                            IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                a joint venture of the other, they are joint ventures of the same third party; one entity is a joint venture of a third
                entity and the other entity is an associate of the third entity; the other entity is a post-employment benefit plan for     IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                the benefit of employees of either the reporting entity or an entity related to the reporting entity; or the entity, or     IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                any member of a group of which it is a part, provides key management personnel services to the reporting entity or          IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                to the parent of the reporting entity.

Definition of an   An associate is an entity over which the investor has significant influence. There is a rebuttable presumption that
associate          an investor that holds an investment, directly and indirectly, of 20 per cent or more of the voting power of the        IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16

method

IAS 29              Financial Reporting in Hyperinflationary Economies                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview                                                                                                                                 IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                    Sets out the requirements for entities reporting in the currency of a hyperinflationary economy.
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Hyperinflation      Generally, an economy is hyperinflationary when the cumulative inflation rate over three years is approaching or
                    exceeds 100 per cent.                                                                                                IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
Change in           When an entity’s functional currency is the currency of a hyperinflationary economy, its financial statements
measurement         are restated so that all amounts are measured at current amounts at the end of the reporting period. The              IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
basis               adjusting gain or loss on the net monetary position is recognised in profit or loss.                                 IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19

                    Comparative figures for prior period(s) are also restated into the same current measuring unit.                      IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
When an economy When an economy ceases to be hyperinflationary, the amounts expressed in the measuring unit current at                   IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
is no longer      the end of the previous reporting period become the basis for the carrying amounts in subsequent financial             IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
hyperinflationary statements.

IAS 32           Financial Instruments: Presentation                                                                                              IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview         Prescribes the accounting for classifying and presenting financial instruments as liabilities or equity and for                  IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                 offsetting financial assets and liabilities.                                                                                     IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Classification   Classification of an instrument is based on its substance rather than its form and the assessment is made at the                 IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                 time of issue and is not altered subsequently.
                                                                                                                                                  IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                 An equity instrument is an instrument that evidences a residual interest in the assets of the entity after deducting              IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                 all of its liabilities. A financial liability is an instrument that obligates an entity to deliver cash or another financial
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                 asset, or the holder has a right to demand cash or another financial asset. Examples are bank loans and trade
                 payables, but also mandatorily redeemable preference shares. Puttable instruments and instruments that impose                    IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
                 on the entity an obligation to deliver a pro-rata share of net assets only on liquidation that are subordinate to all            IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                 other classes of instruments and meet additional criteria, are classified as equity instruments even though they
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                 would otherwise meet the definition of a liability.
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                 An issuer classifies separately the debt and equity components of a single compound instrument such as                           IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                 convertible debt, at the time of issue.
                                                                                                                                                  IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                 The cost of treasury shares is deducted from equity. Resales of treasury shares are accounted for as equity                     IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                 issuances.

                                                                                                                                        IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

                                                                                                                                        IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                   enforceable right to set off the amounts and intends either to settle on a net basis or simultaneously.

                                                                                                                                        IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
of financial       or expense.
performance                                                                                                                             IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2

IAS 33        Earnings per Share                                                                                                       IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
Overview      Sets out the principles for measuring and presenting earnings per share (EPS).
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Scope         Applies to publicly-traded entities, entities in the process of issuing such shares and any other entity voluntarily
              presenting EPS.                                                                                                          IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16

EPS           Requires the presentation of basic and diluted EPS for each class of ordinary share that has a different right to        IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
              share in profit for the period. The measures must be presented with equal prominence.                                     IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10

              EPS is reported for profit or loss attributable to equity holders of the parent entity, for profit or loss from          IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
              continuing operations attributable to equity holders of the parent entity and for any discontinued operations.           IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
              EPS on discontinued operations can be presented in the notes.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
Basic EPS     The numerator is earnings after deduction of all expenses including tax and after deduction of non-
                                                                                                                                       IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
calculation   controlling interests and preference dividends.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
              The denominator is the weighted average number of shares outstanding during the period.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                                                                                                                                       IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                      IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2

                                                                                                                                       Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Diluted EPS       Dilution is a reduction in EPS on the assumption that convertible instruments are converted, that options or
calculation       warrants are exercised or that ordinary shares are issued when specified conditions are met.                         IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                  The numerator is the profit for the period attributable to ordinary shares, increased by the after-tax amount
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                  of dividends and interest recognised in the period in respect of the dilutive potential ordinary shares (such as
                  options, warrants, convertible securities and contingent insurance agreements) and adjusted for any other            IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                  changes in income or expense that would result from the conversion of the dilutive potential ordinary shares.        IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2

                  The denominator is adjusted for the number of shares that would be issued on the conversion of all of the             IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                  dilutive potential ordinary shares into ordinary shares.                                                             IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19

                  Anti-dilutive potential ordinary shares are excluded from the calculation.                                           IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

Interpretations   None                                                                                                                 IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                                                                                                                                       IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32

IAS 34       Interim Financial Reporting                                                                                           IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview     Prescribes the minimum content of an interim financial report and the recognition and measurement                     IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
             principles for an interim financial report.                                                                           IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Scope        An interim financial report is a complete or condensed set of financial statements for a period shorter than an       IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
             entity’s full financial year.
                                                                                                                                   IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
             IAS 34 applies only when an entity is required by a regulator or elects to publish an interim financial report in      IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
             accordance with IFRS Standards.
                                                                                                                                   IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
Content      The minimum components of an interim financial report are condensed versions of the primary financial
                                                                                                                                   IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
             statements.
                                                                                                                                   IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
             The notes in an interim financial report provide an explanation of events and transactions significant to
                                                                                                                                   IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
             understanding the changes since the last annual financial statements. IAS 34 lists specific items that are
             presumed to be necessary in understanding such changes.                                                               IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36

Principles   Materiality is based on interim financial data, not forecast annual amounts.                                          IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                                                                                                                                   IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
             The accounting policies are the same as for the annual report.
                                                                                                                                  IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
             Revenue and costs are recognised when they occur, not if they are anticipated or deferred.

IAS 36        Impairment of Assets                                                                                                          IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview      Sets out requirements to ensure that assets are carried at no more than their recoverable amount and to                       IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
              prescribe how recoverable amount and an impairment loss or its reversal are calculated.                                       IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Scope         IAS 36 applies to assets that are not in the scope of other Standards.                                                        IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16

              Assets that have separate requirements are inventories (IAS 2), contract assets and costs to fulfil a contract                IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
              (IFRS 15), deferred tax assets (IAS 12), assets from employee benefits (IAS 19), financial assets (IFRS 9), investment         IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
              property measured at fair value (IAS 40), biological assets measured at fair value less costs to sell (IAS 41), contracts
                                                                                                                                            IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
              in the scope of IFRS 17 and non-current assets classified as held for sale (IFRS 5).
                                                                                                                                            IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
Identifying   At the end of each reporting period, assets are reviewed to look for any indication that they may be impaired.
impairments                                                                                                                                 IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
              Intangible assets with an indefinite useful life and goodwill must be tested annually irrespective of whether
                                                                                                                                            IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
              there is any indication of impairment.
                                                                                                                                            IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
Recognition   An impairment loss is recognised when the carrying amount of an asset exceeds its recoverable amount.
                                                                                                                                            IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
              An impairment loss is recognised in profit or loss for assets carried at cost and treated as a revaluation
                                                                                                                                            IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
              decrease for assets carried at the revalued amount.
                                                                                                                                           IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
              Reversal of prior years’ impairment losses is required in some cases, but is prohibited for goodwill.

IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                           Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                      Deloitte
                                                                                                                                       IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                            Accounting
                                                                                                                                       Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                       Standards,
                                                                                                                                         Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                       (DART)
                                                                                                                                                           Practice
                                                                                                                                       Statements
                                                                                                                                         Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Recoverable       Recoverable amount is the higher of an asset’s fair value less costs of disposal and its value in use.
amount                                                                                                                                   IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                  Value in use is the present value of estimated future cash flows expected to arise from the continuing use of          IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                  an asset and from its disposal at the end of its useful life. The discount rate used is the pre-tax rate of return
                                                                                                                                         IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                  that investors would require if they were to choose an investment that would generate cash flows equivalent
                  to those expected from the asset. The discount rate must not reflect risks for which future cash flows have            IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                  been adjusted.                                                                                                         IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2

                  Fair value is defined in IFRS 13. Examples for costs of disposal are set out in IAS 36, for example legal costs,        IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                  costs of removing an asset and direct incremental costs to bring an asset into condition for its sale.                 IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
Cash-generating   If it is not possible to determine the recoverable amount for an individual asset, then the recoverable amount         IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
units (CGUs)      of the CGU to which the asset belongs is determined. A CGU is the smallest identifiable group of assets that
                                                                                                                                         IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                  generates cash inflows that are largely independent of the cash inflows from other assets or groups of assets.
Goodwill          The impairment test for goodwill is performed at the lowest level within the entity at which goodwill is               IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                  monitored for internal management purposes, provided that the unit or group of units to which goodwill is              IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                  allocated is not larger than an operating segment as reported in accordance with IFRS 8.                               IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
Interpretations   Refer to IAS 34 for a summary of IFRIC 10 Interim Financial Reporting and Impairment.
                                                                                                                                         IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                        IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2

                                                                                                                                      IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                entities provide to investors about the businesses those entities buy. This includes proposals to require
                entities to disclose management’s objectives for acquisitions in the year of acquisition and how acquisitions         IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                have performed against those objectives in subsequent periods.                                                         IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10

                                                                                                                                      IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27

                    An exposure draft is expected in the first half of 2024.

                                                                                                                                       IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
IAS 37       Provisions, Contingent Liabilities and Contingent Assets                                                                  IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview     Sets out recognition criteria and measurement bases for provisions, contingent liabilities and assets and the             IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
             related disclosure requirements.                                                                                          IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Provisions   A provision is recognised when a past event (the obligation event) has created a legal or constructive obligation,        IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
             an outflow of resources is probable and the amount of the obligation can be estimated reliably. The amount
                                                                                                                                       IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
             recognised is the best estimate of the settlement amount at the end of the reporting period. If the effect of the
             time value of money is material, such as might be the case for restoration or decommissioning costs that must              IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
             be settled well into the future, the provision is measured at the present value of the expenditures expected to be        IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
             required to settle the obligation. The unwinding of the discount is recognised in profit or loss as a finance cost.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
             Provisions are reviewed at the end of each reporting period to adjust for changes in the estimate, for other than the     IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
             time value of money.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
             Planned future expenditure, even when authorised by the board of directors or equivalent governing body, is               IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
             excluded from recognition, as are accruals for self-insured losses, general uncertainties and other events that have      IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
             not yet taken place.
                                                                                                                                       IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
             On a similar basis, future operating losses cannot be recognised as a provision, because there is no obligation at       IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
             the end of the reporting period. The expectation of future operating losses will trigger the need for an impairment
             review (see IAS 36).
                                                                                                                                                           158
IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                               Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                          Deloitte
                                                                                                                                           IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                                Accounting
                                                                                                                                           Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                           Standards,
                                                                                                                                             Research Tool and
                                                                                                                                                           (DART)
                                                                                                                                                               Practice
                                                                                                                                           Statements
                                                                                                                                             Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                             IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Onerous             An executory contract is a contract (or a portion of a contract) that is equally unperformed—neither party
contracts           has fulfilled any of its obligations, or both parties have partially fulfilled their obligations to an equal extent.     IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                    Examples include maintenance or service contracts and employee contracts. The asset and liability are                    IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                    combined so that no asset or liability is recognised in the statement of financial position.                             IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                    An executory contract becomes onerous when the unavoidable costs of meeting the obligations exceed the                   IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                    expected economic benefits from it. This would be the case, for example, when an entity cannot cancel, and               IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                    must continue to pay for, a cleaning contract even though it has vacated the premises to which the contract
                                                                                                                                              IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                    relates. An onerous contract gives rise to a provision. Care must be taken, however, not to include in the
                    provision future operating losses.                                                                                       IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
Contingent          Contingent liabilities are not recognised, but are disclosed, unless the possibility of outflow is remote.               IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
liabilities                                                                                                                                  IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                    They are not recognised because either it is only a possible obligation that is contingent on a future event that
                    is outside the control of the entity or there is a present obligation, but it is not probable that an outflow of         IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                    resources will be required or the amount cannot be measured with sufficient reliability (which will be rare).            IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
Contingent assets   A contingent asset is a possible asset that arises from past events and whose existence will be confirmed only           IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                    by future events not wholly within the control of the entity.
                                                                                                                                             IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                    Contingent assets require disclosure only. If the realisation of income is virtually certain, the related asset is      IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                    not a contingent asset and recognition is required.

                                                                                                                                             IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                  obligating event is the activity that triggers the payment of the levy. If that event occurs over a period of time
                  the liability is recognised progressively. If the levy is triggered on reaching a minimum threshold, the liability is      IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                  recognised when that minimum is reached.                                                                                   IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39

Pending changes The IASB has a standard-setting project to make targeted improvements to IAS 37. The scope of the project
                comprises:                                                                                                            IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                   • Aligning the IAS 37 liability definition and requirements for identifying liabilities with the Conceptual
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                     Framework
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                   • Clarifying which costs to include in the measure of a provision
                                                                                                                                      IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                   • Specifying whether the rate at which an entity discounts a provision for the time value of money should           IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                     reflect the entity’s own credit risk
                                                                                                                                      IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                   The IASB is expected to decide the direction of the project in Q4 of 2023.                                         IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

                   A project on pollutant pricing mechanisms has been added to the IASB’s reserve list. The project will aim to       IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                   develop specific requirements for pollutant pricing mechanisms. Initial research will consider whether the         IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
                   project should aim to address all types of pollutant pricing mechanisms, or only some, such as emission
                                                                                                                                      IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                   trading schemes, and accounting by traders and scheme administrators or limit the project to entities that are
                   required to (or choose to) participate in such schemes.                                                            IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39

IAS 38        Intangible Assets                                                                                                      IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7

Overview      Prescribes the accounting treatment for recognising, measuring and disclosing intangible assets that are not           IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
              addressed in another IFRS Standard.                                                                                    IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
Definition    An intangible asset is an identifiable non-monetary asset without physical substance. Examples include software,       IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
              brands, music and film rights and development assets.
                                                                                                                                     IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
Recognition   Intangible assets are recognised if it is probable that the future economic benefits that are attributable to the
                                                                                                                                      IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
              asset will flow to the entity and the cost of the asset can be measured reliably. There are specific recognition
              criteria for internally-generated intangible assets. All research costs are charged to expense when incurred.          IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
              Development costs are capitalised only after technical and commercial feasibility of the resulting product or          IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
              service have been established.Internally-generated goodwill, brands, mastheads, publishing titles, customer
                                                                                                                                     IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
              lists, start-up costs, training costs, advertising costs and relocation costs are never recognised as assets.
                                                                                                                                     IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
              If an intangible item does not meet the definition and the recognition criteria, the costs are recognised as an
                                                                                                                                     IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
              expense when incurred.
                                                                                                                                     IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
              If an entity recognises a prepayment asset for advertising or promotional expenditure, it is only able to do so
                                                                                                                                     IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
              up to the point at which it has the right to access the goods purchased or up to the point of receipt of services.
              Mail order catalogues are specifically identified as a form of advertising and promotional activities, and are        IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
              expensed when they are received.

IFRS Accounting Standards, Interpretations and                                                                                      Introduction


Practice Statements                                                                                                                 Deloitte
                                                                                                                                  IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                       Accounting
                                                                                                                                  Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                  Standards,

                                                                                                                                                  (DART)
                                                                                                                                                      Practice
                                                                                                                                  Statements
                                                                                                                                    Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                    IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Subsequent    Intangible assets are classified as having either a finite or indefinite life. Indefinite means that there is no
measurement   foreseeable limit to the period over which the asset is expected to generate net cash inflows, not infinite.          IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
              Intangible assets may be accounted for using a cost model or, in limited cases, a revaluation model.                  IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
Cost model    Assets are carried at cost less any accumulated amortisation and any accumulated impairment losses.                   IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13

              Normally, subsequent expenditure on an intangible asset after its purchase or completion is recognised as an          IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
              expense.                                                                                                              IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                                                                                                                                     IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
              The cost of an intangible asset with a finite useful life is amortised over that life, normally to a nil residual
              value. Impairment testing under IAS 36 is required whenever there is an indication that the carrying amount           IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
              exceeds the recoverable amount of the intangible asset.                                                               IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23

              Intangible assets with indefinite useful lives are not amortised but are tested for impairment on an annual           IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
              basis. If the recoverable amount is lower than the carrying amount, an impairment loss is recognised. The             IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
              entity also considers whether the intangible asset continues to have an indefinite life.
                                                                                                                                    IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
                                                                                                                                    IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
                                                                                                                                    IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                                                                                                                                   IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2

Practice Statements                                                                                                                        Deloitte
                                                                                                                                         IFRS       Accounting
                                                                                                                                              Accounting
                                                                                                                                         Interpretations
                                                                                                                                                         Standards,

                                                                                                                                                             Practice
                                                                                                                                         Statements
                                                                                                                                           Our IAS Plus website
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 1    IFRS 2      IFRS 3
Revaluation model If an intangible asset has a quoted market price in an active market, a revaluation model can be used. The
                  asset is carried at fair value at revaluation date less any subsequent amortisation or impairment.                       IFRS 5    IFRS 6      IFRS 7
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 8    IFRS 9     IFRS 10
                    Revaluations must be carried out regularly. When the revaluation model is used, all items of a given class must
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 11   IFRS 12    IFRS 13
                    be revalued. However, if there is no active market for a particular asset within that class that asset is measured
                    using the cost model.                                                                                                  IFRS 14   IFRS 15    IFRS 16
                                                                                                                                           IFRS 17    IAS 1      IAS 2
                    Revaluation increases are recognised in other comprehensive income and accumulated in equity. Revaluation
                    decreases are charged first against the revaluation surplus in equity related to the specific asset, and any            IAS 7     IAS 8      IAS 10
                    excess against profit or loss. When the revalued asset is disposed of, the revaluation surplus remains in equity       IAS 12    IAS 16      IAS 19
                    and is not reclassified to profit or loss.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 20    IAS 21      IAS 23
Interpretations     SIC-32 Intangible Assets—Web Site Costs clarifies which initial infrastructure development and graphic design
                                                                                                                                           IAS 24    IAS 26      IAS 27
                    costs incurred in web site development are capitalised.
                                                                                                                                           IAS 28    IAS 29      IAS 32
Changes             None
effective this                                                                                                                             IAS 33    IAS 34      IAS 36
year                                                                                                                                       IAS 37    IAS 38      IAS 39
Pending changes The IASB has added a project on intangible assets to its research project pipeline. This project will aim to               IAS 40    IAS 41     IFRIC 12
                review comprehensively the accounting requirements for intangible assets. Initial research will seek to identify
                                                                                                                                          IFRIC 17    PS 1           PS 2
                the scope of the project and how best to stage work on this topic to deliver timely improvements to IFRS
                Accounting Standards.
